And the `ns_conj` ↔ two-sidedness correspondence is NOT one-to-one — in `Grp` normality is spent TWICE (`quot_rel_mul` AND `quot_rel_inv`), while in `Rng` two-sidedness is spent ONCE (`rquot_rel_mul` only), additive commutativity handling the negation congruence. **One-sidedness is REFUTED, not assumed**: `Instance/Rng/Quotient/OneSided.v` exhibits `E11Left : LeftIdeal UT2` (the column ℤ·E₁₁ in the upper-triangular 2×2 ring) and `ut2_left_ideal_not_mul_congruence` refutes **the congruence itself** — the property `rquot_rel_mul` establishes and the construction actually consumes — where #313's `S3_refl_sub_not_normal` refutes the closure condition. **Read that comparison precisely — an audit corrected an earlier overclaim here**: the group side ALSO carries a congruence-level refutation at the same subgroup, `Instance/Grp/Congruence.v:1009`'s `S3_refl_sub_no_congruence` (landed by #301), so it is NOT the case that the group side leaves an inference to the reader; what is actually different is only that the ring refutation is DIRECT where the group one goes through `congruence_iff_normal`, and that it lands on the MULTIPLICATION congruence specifically, the clause one-sidedness cannot supply. `ut2_e11_not_right_absorbing` and `E11Left_is_not_an_Ideal` separately refute the dropped closure, and the positive half at the SAME ring is `StrictUpper`, a proper nontrivial two-sided ideal whose quotient does not collapse and **is commutative though UT2 is not**. Both universal elements are over `Theory/Universal/Element.v`'s class with the underlying arrow the projection and the extracted mediator the direct one, both by `eq_refl`. **The three module isomorphism theorems are GRADED, not claimed uniform**, following #313's discipline: the THIRD is a pure universal-property chase (its mediator lemmas destructure nothing; the only element computation is `TmodS`'s saturation obligation, a prerequisite to STATING the theorem); the FIRST derives its comparison from `universal_element_iso` but `mod_image_med` DESTRUCTURES an element, reading the preimage out of the image witness (data, so nothing is chosen), and its uniqueness clause is proved; the SECOND is a corollary composed with `mod_surjective_image_iso` and `mquot_congr`, which ARE hand-built inverse pairs with round trips — degenerate, identity legs, but not instances of `universal_element_iso` — and has no uniqueness clause. **No proof anywhere manipulates a coset**, the setoid quotient forming no coset object, and the header says that is a property of the presentation. **`RModQuotient` is RECONCILED rather than left as a second unshared construction**: `ImageSubmod f : Submodule N`, `rmod_quotient_relations_agree` a biconditional at every pair, and `RModQuotient_is_quotient_by_image : RModQuotient f ≅[RMod R] QuotientMod (ImageSubmod f)` with both legs the identity on elements — the two relations being `{a & x ≈ y + f a}` versus `{a & x − y ≈ f a}`, NOT convertible (pinned as a `Fail` probe), the shuffle between them being the biconditional's whole content. **`AbQuotient` is deliberately NOT unified, and the dependency argument runs OPPOSITE to the one #313 recorded**: `Instance/Ab.v` is strictly UPSTREAM of `Instance/Mod.v` (Mod requires Ab, and `RModQuotient` is literally built on `AbQuotient`), so routing it through a submodule quotient would INVERT an existing dependency — the reason not only still applies but applies with more force, and the honest unifier would be an `AbSubgroup` interface in `Instance/Ab.v` of which `Submodule` is the extension, recorded but not built. `Instance/Ab/Subtract.v` is the shared subtraction calculus, factored so no ring file depends on a module file for it and labelled an upstreaming staging post rather than the first word (`ab_neg_invol` is knowingly its THIRD copy in tree). **A name-collision hazard was found and fixed**: 34 names collided with `Instance/Grp/Quotient/Isomorphism.v`, and since the gate Requires both in one scope, `Print Assumptions first_isomorphism_theorem` would silently have audited the WRONG constant — the module side is renamed `mod_*`/`ImageSubmod`, and zero collisions remain among the four quotient files. Non-vacuity over ℤ throughout (2ℤ proper and nontrivial; 2ℤ ⊃ 4ℤ with the middle quotient proper AND nontrivial for the third theorem; 2ℤ and 3ℤ incomparable with `1 ∈ 2ℤ+3ℤ` for the second) and the ring UMP EXERCISED, the mediator ℤ/6ℤ → ℤ/2ℤ being the class-produced one by `eq_refl`. Deferred: the RING isomorphism theorems (the image of a ring hom is a SUBRING, not an ideal, needing a `Subring` interface the issue does not ask for); no sum/product/intersection of ideals, no principal/prime/maximal ideals, no CRT; no identification of ℤ/2ℤ with `F2_Ring`; no `HasCokernels`; no submodule lattice, correspondence theorem, modular law or exact sequences; no naturality of any isomorphism
- **Instance/Discrete.v**: Discrete categories, and Mac Lane §I.2's "a discrete category is determined by its set of objects" — `DiscreteCat A` (objects `A`, morphisms equality proofs) with `DiscreteCat_Discrete` one way, and the reconstruction the other in Instance/Discrete/Reconstruct.v: `Discrete_iso : Discrete C → DiscreteRigid C → C ≅[StrictCat] DiscreteCat obj[C]`, with the `Cat` reading alongside. `DiscreteRigid` is the extra hypothesis and it is stated INTRINSICALLY — the comparison functor is faithful (`DiscreteRigid_Faithful`) — with UIP on objects and decidable object equality as sufficient conditions (`DiscreteRigid_UIP`, `DiscreteRigid_dec`, the latter by Hedberg, axiom-free). It is not an artifact of the proof: `DiscreteCat`'s homset is strict `eq` on equality proofs, so a functor into it must identify parallel proofs. The file also addresses `Structure/Discrete.v`'s long-standing "Equality is too much here" caveat with the iso-robust `DiscreteUpToIso` (subsingleton homs, all arrows invertible), and then shows the two notions are genuinely independent rather than merely differently phrased: `Discrete_DiscreteUpToIso_forces_UIP` proves that `∀ C, Discrete C → DiscreteUpToIso C` would entail UIP for *every* type, and `Indiscrete_bool_Discrete_absurd` with `Indiscrete_DiscreteUpToIso` exhibits a category that is discrete-up-to-iso but not `Discrete`. Functor categories OVER discrete shapes are Instance/Fun/Discrete.v (Mac Lane §II.4): transformations over a discrete domain are bare families with naturality free (`Discrete_Transform`, with the hom-setoid isomorphism `Discrete_hom_iso : (F ⟹ G) ≅[Sets] (∀ a, F a ~> G a)`), `[DiscreteCat A, B] ≅[Cat] PiCat (fun _ => B)` for ANY index type, the finite power `Fun_Discrete_power : [DiscreteCat (Fin.t n), B] ≅ Pow B n` by Fin-peeling entirely at PiCat level (`Pow` carries a trailing terminal factor as its nullary case — n B-factors ∏ `1` — with the literal `B ∏ B` shape exhibited at n = 2 via `prod_one_r`), the power-set computation `Fun_Discrete_bool_subsets` (functors into `DiscreteCat bool` up to natural iso = characteristic functions `X → bool`, the constructive decidable-subsets reading), the axiom-free Cantor diagonal `cantor_predicates`/`cantor_bool` with `Fun_Discrete_no_surjection` connecting it to the correspondence — the size half of Mac Lane's remark, with the universe-sized half rendered structurally by the library's universe discipline — and Seven Sketches 3.53's morphism half `One_hom_iso` (hom-setoid iso in Sets, any target, instantiated at Sets; the object half `[1, C] ≅ C` is Theory/Shapes.v's `One_Fun_iso`, cited not rebuilt)
- **Instance/Cat.v**: Category of categories
- **Instance/Fun.v**: Functor categories [C, D]
- **Instance/Fun/Terminal.v**: Mac Lane §III.5 Ex 5 — the POINTWISE terminal object and pointwise INDEXED products of a functor category, the nullary and infinitary halves of an exercise whose binary half was already `Functor_Category_Cartesian` (`Instance/Fun/Cartesian.v:111`). **`Functor_Category_Terminal (T : @Terminal D) : @Terminal ([C, D])`** — the constant functor at `1` with the unique natural transformation into it, registered as an `#[export] Program Instance` on the binary case's precedent, and named so because **issue #718 (Awodey §8.7, exponentials of presheaves) records that it DEFERS the terminal presheaf to this issue** — a fact about the issue tracker, not about the tree: no in-tree consumer exists yet, the probe being the only importer, and that is stated rather than implied. **`Fun_HasIndexedProducts : HasIndexedProducts D → HasIndexedProducts ([C, D])` at an ARBITRARY index `Type`**, built ELEMENTARY-FIRST — `Fun_iprod`/`Fun_iprod_proj`/`Fun_IsIndexedProduct` at the apex-pinned `IsIndexedProduct`, then `Build_HasIndexedProducts` — with **no `Limit`, no `Cone` and no `DiscreteCat` occurring anywhere in the file**, deliberately (see the universe correction below). It is a plain `Definition` and NOT an `Instance`, a disclosed design call rather than an oversight: it carries a premise of its own class, so registering it would let resolution unify an unknown category with `Fun ?C ?D` and recurse, where the leaf `Sets_HasIndexedProducts` is safe to register. **A UNIVERSE ATTRIBUTION THIS INDEX HAD CARRIED WRONGLY IS CORRECTED HERE, AND THE CORRECTION IS MEASURED THREE WAYS**: `DiscreteCat_Functor` ALONE does NOT pin the ambient category — `probe_functor_only` has `C : Category@{u0 u2 u2}`, FREE, the functor fixing only the SHAPE at `DiscreteCat@{u Set Set}` — and `Cone` is NOT a co-donor either, `probe_cone_of_discrete` being free at `C : Category@{u1 u2 u2}`; the ambient `Set` pin needs a SECOND donor that identifies the shape's hom and proof with the ambient's, giving `probe_limit_of_discrete` at `C : Category@{u1 Set Set}`. **It takes BOTH.** Read the `Cone` half NARROWLY: the RECORD is innocent, but that licenses no claim that `IsALimit`/`Limit` are the ONLY other donors, and they are not — `cone_leg` (`Structure/Limit/Preservation.v:108`) and `IsLimitCone` (`:166`) identify them the same way, so CONE VOCABULARY is among the donors even though the record is not; `Structure/Limit/Initial.v`'s own bullet already warns that an `ACone` control rules out only `ACone`/`Cone`, and an earlier revision of this bullet ran that control and drew the broader conclusion anyway. The practical conclusion every affected bullet draws — do not route an indexed product through the discrete presentation — is UNCHANGED; only the `Construction/Coproduct/Indexed.v`/`Instance/Cat/Coproduct.v` bullet stated the mechanism as one donor, and that is the one fixed in the same commit — an earlier revision of THIS sentence misnamed it as `Structure/Bicartesian/Matrix.v`'s, which does not mention `DiscreteCat_Functor` at all; `Functor/Hom/Limit.v`'s named both donors and was right, and `Structure/Limit/Product/Finite.v`'s cites the composite and is sound. **A SECOND MEASUREMENT LESSON, and it inverts the house rule**: `Functor_Category_Terminal@{u u0 u1 u2 u3 u4}`'s constraint block is SIX BOUNDS WITH NO EQUATION, yet its binder reads `C : Category@{u2 u4 u4}` and `D : Category@{u3 u4 u4}` — hom identified with proof in BOTH and the two identified with each other, expressed by REUSING the level variable. Reading the block alone reports "no identification" and is WRONG; both must be read. That identification is `Fun`'s, and `Fun` does more than its four sibling donors: **FIVE donors identify hom with proof INDEPENDENTLY** — `Terminal`, `Cartesian`, `HasIndexedProducts`, `IsIndexedProduct` and `Fun` — each rejected under `Constraint uh < up` against controls naming a hom and an object family at those very levels, so no single one is "the" cause. What stays FREE is worth as much: `Constant_Functor@{co ch cp dro dh dp}` identifies NOTHING (five bounds, `Functor`'s own block verbatim) and `Constant_Terminal_Functor` keeps **C's hom and proof APART** — which is why the constant functor is built by hand rather than cited from `Functor/Diagonal.v`, `fobj[@Diagonal D C] d` being REJECTED at `C : Category@{co ch cp}` with `ch < cp` ("Cannot enforce cp = dh") against the hand-built functor as a passing control at the same levels; both records are compared in-file anyway, agreeing on BOTH actions at `eq_refl` while the whole records are refuted, `Diagonal` being a `Program Instance` with opaque law fields. `Fun_iprod` keeps C's and D's hom levels DISTINCT and leaves the INDEX universe only BOUNDED, never identified; the file's only EQUATION is `Fun`'s `u0 = u2`, carried by EIGHT constants rather than the one an earlier revision of this bullet named — `Fun_IsIndexedProduct` plus `fun_terminal_obj`, `fun_terminal_fmap`, `fun_terminal_one_component`, `fun_iprod_tuple`, `constant_functor_diagonal_obj`, `constant_functor_diagonal_fmap` and `ctl_bool_iprod_obj`, every constant whose statement is at `[C, D]`; it is the SAME equation each time so the conclusion survives, but the sentence read as a count; and **the general theorems carry no `Set` at all**, the witnesses' `Set` being the SHAPE's (`Instance/Two.v` declares `TwoHom : … → Set`). **The binary comparison is an ISOMORPHISM and NOT `eq_refl`, and the reason is mathematical rather than presentational**: `HasIndexedProducts D` and `Cartesian D` choose their objects INDEPENDENTLY, so `fobj[Fun_iprod HP (bool_fam F G)] c = fobj[F × G] c` is refuted (pinned in-file with controls) and `Fun_bool_iprod_iso` with BOTH leg families is the strongest available; `cartesian_bool_IsIndexedProduct` supplies the generic half — in ANY cartesian category the binary product IS an indexed product over `bool`. whether some other natural isomorphism exists there is neither proved nor refuted. **Universes measured off BOTH binder and block**: every section constant is over `C : Category@{u u0 u0}`, `D : Category@{u1 u2 u2}` — hom identified with proof in the BINDER — with the one block equation `u0 = u2` (42 of the 56 blocks carry it and no other equation occurs; 27 heads and no `Program`; no `Record`/`Class`/`Inductive` in either file, so no `Build_*`), the gate carrying all 83 fully qualified; zero collisions over 96 names after one was renamed before landing (`op_unit_is_counit` is `Adjunction/FullFaithful.v:599`'s, hence `fixed_op_unit_is_counit`); - **Instance/Pos.v**: `Pos`, the category of posets and monotone maps (Awodey §1.4) — the STRUCTURED-SETS reading, whose OBJECTS are posets, as distinct from `Instance/Poset.v` which turns ONE poset into a thin category. Neither subsumes the other. `Instance/Poset.v` cannot be reused as the donor: its `Poset` (:118-119) is `Definition Poset ... := Proset P`, DISCARDING antisymmetry, so a `Poset` value *is* a `Proset` value and the structure has to be bundled — which `PosetObject` does. It carries a field `Instance/Poset.v` lacks — `pos_le_respects`, the order being compatible with the carrier's own `≈` — which is an object WELL-FORMEDNESS condition, not consumed by any proof below (the file compiles without it); it is kept for the same reason `Instance/CMon.v` carries `cmon_plus_respects`. `pos_antisym` concludes `x ≈ y`, not Leibniz equality — that is what makes this the category of POSETS (the category itself compiles without it), and a disclosed deviation from Awodey's set-level statement. Morphisms are `MonoHom` (a `SetoidMorphism` plus monotonicity), compared pointwise up to `≈`; Awodey's two closure facts (identities monotone, composites monotone) are the discharged obligations of `mono_hom_id`/`mono_hom_compose`. Plus `Pos_Forget : Pos ⟶ Sets`, and the bridge to the other reading — `PosetAsCategory` sends a poset to its thin category and `MonotoneAsFunctor` a monotone map to the induced functor. NOTE #641's "alternative" (realize `Pos` as a full subcategory of `Cat` on "the skeletal thin categories `Poset P`") was not taken, for the reason the issue itself gives — it calls the direct construction the recommended principal artifact. The subcategory is in fact constructible in a few lines (`Construction/Subcategory.v:32`'s `sobj : C → Type` takes any `Type`-valued predicate, needing no equality on `Category` values), and "skeletal thin" does NOT misdescribe its family — skeletality is what antisymmetry buys and strictly excludes preorders. What is genuinely deferred is the AGREEMENT between the two presentations, which needs the carrier setoid and order recovered from a skeletal thin category.
- **Instance/Ord.v**, **Instance/Ord/Poset.v**: Mac Lane §IV.3 Exercise 4(a) (book p. 92, `maclane:IV.3:ex4`) — **the category `Ord` of ALL preorders (Mac Lane's Preord) and its full reflective subcategory of partial orders**, delivered as `Poset_Reflective_in_Ord : Reflective Pos_Sub` (`Construction/Reflective.v:60`'s record: `Pos_Sub_Full`, `Poset_reflector`, `Poset_adj`). **THE ISSUE'S "Current state" IS STALE IN ONE DIRECTION AND RIGHT IN THE OTHER**: it says "both ambient categories are missing", whereas `Instance/Pos.v:150`'s `Pos` (the category whose OBJECTS are posets, #641) and `Instance/Top.v:273`'s `Top` (#259) both exist at `d658518e`; what was genuinely absent is a category of ALL preorders — `rg -i 'PreorderObject|ProsetObject|OrdObject|OrdHom'` returned zero hits, and the only in-tree "preorder as a category" is `Instance/Proset.v:35`'s `Proset P`, which turns ONE preorder into a thin category — which is exactly the reviewer check on this issue, and it is delivered as CONSTRUCTIONS rather than prose: `Ord_obj_are_preorders : obj[Ord] = OrdObject` and `OrdAsCategory_obj_are_points : obj[OrdAsCategory P] = carrier …` both by `eq_refl`, their identification refuted in the probe, and the one-way passage `OrdAsCategory`/`OrdHomAsFunctor` mirroring `Instance/Pos.v:191/:197`. **The name is `Ord` because the issue pins `Poset_Reflective_in_Ord`**, and the tree's own prose had anticipated "an Ord category"; the two sentences this makes false — `Instance/Proset.v:20-22` ("does not exist yet; it is #786") and `Instance/Roster.v:140` ("Mac Lane's Preord is `Proset`", which was never right) — are corrected in the same commit, and #786 (Seven Sketches' preorder/Bool-category equivalence) now consumes `Ord` rather than building it. `OrdObject` is `PosetObject` (`Instance/Pos.v:81`) MINUS `pos_antisym`, field for field, and `OrdHom` is `MonoHom` (`:99`) — deliberately, so that the comparison with the pre-existing `Pos` is cheap: `OrdObject_of_Poset` drops the field, `Poset_of_antisym` supplies it, BOTH object round trips close by `eq_refl` on the WHOLE record (primitive-projection eta), and **`Pos_Posets_strict_iso : @Isomorphism StrictCat Pos Posets` is an ISOMORPHISM OF CATEGORIES** (`Posets := Sub Ord Pos_Sub`), not merely an equivalence — one `eq_on_obj` family is `fun P => eq_refl` outright, the other needs one `destruct` because an object of `Posets` is a stdlib `sigT`, which `Lib.v:10`'s `Set Primitive Projections` does not cover, so `(`1 x; `2 x) = x` is not definitional (pinned, with the SAME statement proved by `posets_pos_posets_obj` as the control, so the negative measures the missing eta and nothing else). The reflection is the #371 design: `PosetReflection P` has the SAME carrier and the SAME order (both `eq_refl`) and differs from `P` in ONE field, its `≈` coarsened to `ord_le P x y ∧ ord_le P y x` — a setoid quotient with no equivalence class, no quotient axiom and no choice principle — so antisymmetry of the reflection is the DEFINITION of its `≈`, the projection is the identity on points, and the only new argument is the mediator's respectfulness (monotonicity twice, then the TARGET's antisymmetry, the one place membership in the subcategory is spent); the route is `universal_arrow_from_UMP` (`Theory/Universal/Arrow.v:158`) → `LeftAdjointFunctorFromUniversalArrows` → `AdjunctionFromUniversalArrows`, nothing re-derived, and `poset_reflect_iso` instantiates `reflective_counit_iso`. Reviewer check on the unit at its true strength: `poset_unit_is_proj` POINTWISE at `eq_refl`, `≈` as a morphism record (the class unit is the composite `fmap[Incl] id ∘ reflection_proj`), the same grade #371 and `Instance/Mod/Free.v:542` have. Witnesses: `Chaos2` (`bool` under the TOTAL relation, not antisymmetric by discrimination, its reflection merging the two points), `NatLe` (antisymmetric, so `natle_reflect_iso` applies and the reflection is INERT — `PosetReflection NatLe = NatLe` refuted at `eq_refl` while isomorphic, the two differing only in the setoid field), and the MIXED three-point `MixOrd` merging exactly one pair and keeping the top apart by direct computation of the relation. **A false pass caught and recorded rather than quietly fixed**: `Fail Example : obj[Ord] := OrdAsCategory P` SUCCEEDS, because Coq's `reverse_coercion` silently accepts the ascription through `OrdObject`'s coercion to `Type`; the shipped negative compares the two object TYPES instead. Universes: NO constraint block of the 122 constants carries an equation; `Ord@{…} : Category@{u u4 u4}` identifies hom with proof in the BINDER, with a binder and block character-for-character identical to `Pos`'s (measured, hence inherited from the template); `Subcategory`'s identification is guarded by one formability negative (`Reflective` cannot be tested apart from its `Subcategory` argument, the #340 `MonoidObject` trap); 113 of the 122 carry `Set` in a universe instance or constraint block, always as a BOUND, attributed by a discriminating experiment to the `Prop`-valued order (the same two-field record with `Type` in place of `Prop` carries none). 122/122 closed under the global context (Ord 64 + `Build_OrdObject`/`Build_OrdHom` invisible to both glob and `Print Module` — which WRAPS the `Record` keyword and misses `OrdHom` on a line-anchored sweep — and Poset 56 (40 glob heads + 12 `Program` obligations + 4 `MixPt` eliminators)); closures 20 and 32 (transitive in-project `.vo` deps excluding the file); zero collisions; `Test/ProbeOrd372.v` carries 7 `Fail`s (4 conversion, 1 typing, 1 formability, 1 instrument), each stripped one at a time, rename-simulated 12/13 with `Ord` itself `Check`-guarded (a whole-word rename rewrites the module path). NOT delivered: no passage from a thin category back to an `OrdObject`, no bridge to `Instance/Proset/Skeletal.v`'s `Proset_Skeleton` (the thin-category shadow of this reflection), no relation to `MonotoneFun`/`TwoPreorder`, no limits or completeness for `Ord`, no idempotent monad, no coreflection, and no specialization preorder of a space — the natural bridge to clause (b), built in neither file

### Applied Programming (Theory/Coq/)
Bridges pure theory with practical Coq programming:
- Applicative functors proven via monoidal functors
- Traversable functors with laws
- Monad transformers with coherence conditions — `MonadTransformer` and `MonadTransformerLaws` are in **Monad/Transformer.v**, NOT under `Theory/Coq/`; they are listed here because they belong to this applied layer thematically

## Critical Design Patterns

### Equivalence Over Equality
Never use `=` for morphisms. Always use `≈`:
```coq
(* WRONG *)
Lemma foo : f ∘ id = f.

(* RIGHT *)
Lemma foo : f ∘ id ≈ f.
```

### Proper Morphisms
All operations must respect equivalence:
```coq
Program Instance Foo_Proper {C : Category} :
  Proper (equiv ==> equiv ==> equiv) (@foo C).
```

### Notation Precedence
- `~>` morphisms (level 90)
- `∘` composition (level 40, left associative)
- `⟶` functors between categories
- `⟹` natural transformations

### Proof Automation
Standard proof pattern:
```coq
Proof.
  intros.
  cat.          (* tries simplification + rewriting *)
  proper.       (* if proving Proper *)
  equivalence.  (* if proving Equivalence *)
Qed.
```

## Common Development Tasks

### Adding a New Category Instance
1. Define objects and morphisms
2. Define equivalence relation on morphisms (setoid)
3. Prove it forms a category (id, compose, laws)
4. Place in Instance/ directory
5. Add to _CoqProject

### Proving Functoriality
1. Define object mapping F_obj
2. Define morphism mapping F_map
3. Prove F_map respects equivalence
4. Prove F preserves id and composition
5. Use `Program Instance` to manage obligations

### Establishing Adjunctions
1. Define functors F : C ⟶ D and G : D ⟶ C
2. Define unit η : Id ⟹ G ○ F
3. Define counit ε : F ○ G ⟹ Id
4. Prove triangle identities
5. Or use hom-set adjunction definition

## References and Learning

When working with specific concepts, reference:
- **nLab**: https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/[concept_name]
- **README.md**: Contains detailed notation guide (the "Notations" section)
- **docs/AXIOMS.md**: the axiom audit — what each `Axiom`/`Parameter` is, which definitions are certified "Closed under the global context", and which stdlib axioms the concrete instance layers do use.
- **docs/INHABITATION.md**: which headline results carry a concrete in-tree witness of their distinctive premise, and which are proven parametrically as axiom-free conditionals awaiting a model. Consult it before citing a flagship theorem (GAFT, SAFT, the Sheaf theory, `image_mediator_epic`, the spider results, …) as demonstrated over a concrete object — several are conditional-only.
- **Key Files and Concepts** (above): the per-module index of the library's headline developments
- **In-file background essays**: beyond the definitional header most files carry, the flagship concept files across Theory/, Structure/, Construction/, Instance/, Adjunction/, and the Comonad development open with a background essay — the concept's history, purpose, cross-disciplinary use, and in-tree connections, each substantive claim cited to a primary source (nLab, the named papers, textbooks, or package documentation). Read that block first when approaching an unfamiliar file.

## Versioning

Default version: Rocq 9.1 (the `default` package in flake.nix)
Supported: Coq 8.19-8.20, Rocq 9.0-9.1
Equations dependency required for some parts (versions matched to Coq)

## Testing Individual Theorems

To test a specific construction:
```bash
# Extract just the files needed
coqdep -R . Category Theory/MyConstruction.v | grep -v "^#"

# Compile dependencies first
make Theory/Category.vo Theory/Functor.vo

# Then compile your file
coqc -R . Category Theory/MyConstruction.v
```
